Fight Night: Machida vs. Romero]]\n|followingevent=[[The Ultimate Fighter: American Top Team vs. Blackzilians#The Ultimate Fighter 21 Finale|The Ultimate Fighter: American Top Team vs. Blackzilians Finale]]\n}}\n'''''UFC 189: Mendes vs. McGregor''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held on July 11, 2015, at the [[MGM Grand Garden Arena]] in [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], Nevada.{{cite news|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/Aldo-McGregor-Set-for-International-Fight-Week-in-July|title=Aldo-McGregor set for International Fight Week in July|publisher=ufc.com|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=2015-01-30|access-date=2015-01-30}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event took place during the UFC's annual International Fight Week.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2014/09/ufc-189-set-for-july-11-in-las-vegas-following-2015-international-fight-week|title=UFC 189 set for July 11 in Las Vegas following 2015 'International Fight Week'|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Matt Erickson|date=2014-09-25|access-date=2015-01-31}}\n\nThe event was expected to be headlined by a [[UFC Featherweight Championship]] bout between the then champion [[José Aldo]] and [[Conor McGregor]]. On June 23, it was reported that Aldo suffered a [[rib fracture]] and pulled out of the bout in light of the injury.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/06/jose-aldo-injured-questionable-for-ufc-189-bout-with-conor-mcgregor|title=Champ Jose Aldo injured, questionable for UFC 189 bout with Conor McGregor|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-06-23|access-date=2015-06-24}} On June 30, it was confirmed that Aldo indeed pulled out of the bout and that the interim championship bout would take place at this event. The bout remained the event headliner.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/06/jose-aldo-officially-out-of-ufc-189-chad-mendes-meets-conor-mcgregor-for-interim-belt|title=Jose Aldo officially out of UFC 189; Chad Mendes meets Conor McGregor for interim belt|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Matt Erickson|date=2015-06-30|access-date=2015-06-30}} On July 1, photographs of Aldo's scans were released and indicated that he had indeed suffered a fractured rib.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/07/heres-a-picture-of-the-rib-that-forced-champ-jose-aldo-out-of-ufc-189|title=Here's a picture of the rib that forced champ Jose Aldo out of UFC 189|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-07-01|access-date=2015-07-02}} On July 10, White claimed that Aldo did not have a broken rib as \"every x-ray he sent out was of an old injury\". He reaffirmed that Aldo had a \"bruised rib and cartilage\" and that the biggest problem was the weight cutting process being complicated by the injury. They later fought at [[UFC 194]].{{cite news|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2015/7/10/8930591/dana-white-jose-aldo-did-not-have-a-broken-rib-the-x-rays-he-released|title=Dana White: Jose Aldo 'did not have a broken rib,' the x-rays he released were of an 'old injury'.|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Shaun Al-Shatti|date=2015-07-10|access-date=2015-07-10}}\n\nThe co-main event featured a [[UFC Welterweight Championship]] bout between then champion [[Robbie Lawler]] and top contender [[Rory MacDonald (fighter)|Rory MacDonald]].{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/02/champ-robbie-lawler-meets-rory-macdonald-at-ufc-189-as-events-second-title-fight|title=Champ Robbie Lawler meets Rory MacDonald at UFC 189 as event's second title fight|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-02-18|access-date=2015-02-18}} Their first fight at [[UFC 167]] ended in a split decision victory for Lawler.{{cite web|url=http://www.middleeasy.com/fighting/item/12369-robbie-lawler-beats-up-rory-macdonald-and-nabs-the-split-decision|title=Robbie Lawler beats up Rory MacDonald and nabs the split-decision|publisher=middleasy.com|author=Jason Nawara |date=2013-11-17|access-date=2015-02-18}}\n\n[[Matt Brown (fighter)|Matt Brown]] was briefly linked to a bout with [[Nate Diaz]] at the event.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/04/nate-diaz-returns-to-welterweight-meets-matt-brown-on-stacked-ufc-189-in-las-vegas|title=Nate Diaz returns to welterweight, meets Matt Brown on stacked UFC 189 in Las Vegas|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-04-08|access-date=2015-04-08}} However, in mid-April, Brown announced that the pairing had been scrapped.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/04/matt-brown-says-hes-no-longer-fighting-nate-diaz-at-ufc-189|title=Matt Brown says he's no longer fighting Nate Diaz at UFC 189|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-04-15|access-date=2015-04-15}} Brown remained on the card against [[Tim Means]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/04/matt-brown-vs-tim-means-added-to-ufc-189|title=Matt Brown vs. Tim Means added to UFC 189|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-04-21|access-date=2015-04-21}}\n\nA welterweight bout between [[Jake Ellenberger]] and [[Stephen Thompson (fighter)|Stephen Thompson]] was initially expected to take place at this event. However, in mid-May, the bout was made the main event of [[The Ultimate Fighter: American Top Team vs. Blackzilians#The Ultimate Fighter 21 Finale|The Ultimate Fighter: American Top Team vs. Blackzilians Finale]] which took place a day later at the same venue.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/05/ufc-fight-night-70-moves-to-florida-gets-machida-vs-romero-tuf-21-finale-set-for-vegas|title=UFC Fight Night 70 moves to Florida, gets Machida-Romero; TUF 21 Finale set for Vegas|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-05-15|access-date=2015-05-15}}\n\n[[John Hathaway (English wrestler)|John Hathaway]] was expected to face [[Gunnar Nelson (fighter)|Gunnar Nelson]] at the event. However, on June 23, Hathaway pulled out of the bout due to an injury. This prompted a shift in another welterweight bout on the card as [[Brandon Thatch]] was pulled from his fight against [[John Howard (fighter)|John Howard]] to face Nelson, while [[Cathal Pendred]] was announced as Howard's new opponent. Pendred had previously fought only 28 days before the event, when he defeated [[Augusto Montaño]] at [[UFC 188]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/Welterweight-Shuffle-at-UFC-189|title=Welterweight shuffle at UFC 189|publisher=ufc.com|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=2015-06-23|access-date=2015-06-23}}\n\n[[Jeremy Stephens]] missed weight on his first attempt, coming in 3.5 lb overweight at 149.5 lb. After having made no attempts to cut further, he was fined 20 percent of his fight purse, which went to [[Dennis Bermudez]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/ufc-189-weigh-in-results-mendes-vs-mcgregor-make-weight-then-pulled-apart|title=UFC 189 weigh-in results: Mendes vs. McGregor make weight then pulled apart|publisher=mmaweekly.com|author=Staff|date=2015-07-11|access-date=2015-07-11}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Featherweight\n|[[Conor end|notes=yes}}\n{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC189|title=UFC 189: Mendes vs. McGregor|publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship|access-date=2015-06-30}}\n\n==Bonus awards==\nThe following fighters were awarded $50,000 bonuses:{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/07/ufc-189-bonuses-fight-of-the-night-its-dana-whites-fight-of-the-ever|title=UFC 189 bonuses: 'Fight of the Night'? It's Dana White's 'Fight of the … Ever'|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Matt Erickson|date=2015-07-12|access-date=2015-07-12}}\n*'''Fight of the Night: Robbie Lawler vs. Rory MacDonald'''\n*'''Performance of the Night: Conor McGregor''' and '''Thomas Almeida'''\n\n==Reported payout==\nThe following is the reported payout to the fighters as reported to the [[Nevada State Athletic Commission]]. It does not include sponsor money and also does not include the UFC's traditional \"fight night\" bonuses.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/07/ufc-189-salaries-conor-mcgregor-and-chad-mendes-both-earn-flat-500k|title=UFC 189 salaries: Conor McGregor and Chad Mendes both earn flat $500K|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=2015-07-13|access-date=2015-07-13|author=Staff}}\n* '''Conor McGregor:''' $500,000 (no win bonus) def. Chad Mendes: $500,000\n* '''Robbie Lawler:''' $300,000 (includes $150,000 win bonus) def. Rory MacDonald: $59,000\n* '''Jeremy Stephens:''' $72,000 (includes $40,000 win bonus) def. Dennis Bermudez: $34,000 ^\n* '''Gunnar Nelson:''' $58,000 (includes $29,000 win bonus) def. Brandon Thatch: $22,000\n* '''Thomas Almeida:''' $24,000 (includes $12,000 win bonus) def. Brad Pickett: $30,000\n* '''Matt Brown:''' $92,000 (includes $46,000 win bonus) def. Tim Means: $23,000\n* '''Alex Garcia:''' $30,000 (includes $15,000 win bonus) def. Mike Swick: $48,000\n* '''John Howard:''' $42,000 (includes $21,000 win bonus) def. Cathal Pendred: $10,000\n* '''Cody Garbrandt:''' $20,000 (includes $10,000 win bonus) def. Henry Briones: $10,000\n* '''Louis Smolka:''' $30,000 (includes $15,000 win bonus) def. Neil Seery: $10,000\n* '''Cody Pfister:''' $20,000 (includes $10,000 win bonus) def. Yosdenis Cedeno: $13,000\n\n^ ''Jeremy Stephens was fined 20 percent of his purse ($8,000) for failing to make the required weight for his fight with Dennis Bermudez. That money was issued to Bermudez, an NSAC official confirmed.''\n\n==Records set==\nThe event's weigh-ins took place before a record crowd estimated at 11,500.\n\nThe event had a $7,200,000 gate, which broke the record for a mixed martial arts event in the United States. The final attendance for the event was 16,019, a record for [[Nevada]], which had hosted 91 prior UFC events.\n\n==Lawler vs. MacDonald II==\nThe rematch between Robbie Lawler and Rory MacDonald for the welterweight championship was named 2015's Fight of the Year by [[Sherdog]]{{cite web |last=Martin |first=Todd |title=Sherdog.com's 2015 Fight of the Year - Lawler vs. MacDonald |url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/1/Sherdogcoms-2015-Fight-of-the-Year-97893 |website=Sherdog |access-date=13 June 2022 |language=en |date=31 December 2015}} and [[MMA Fighting]].{{cite web |last1=Raimondi |first1=Marc |title=2015 Fight of the Year: Lawler vs. MacDonald |url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2015/12/29/10650846/2015-fight-of-the-year-robbie-lawler-vs-rory-macdonald-ufc-189 |website=MMA Fighting |access-date=13 June 2022 |language=en |date=29 December 2015}} The fight was exceptionally brutal - Lawler's lip was deeply lacerated by the end of the fight and MacDonald's nose bled profusely after being broken by the end of the first round, leading him to fight with what has been described as a \"crimson mask\" of blood.{{Cite web |last=Davidson |first=Neil |date=2023-04-09 |title=Canada's Rory MacDonald headed to UFC Hall of Fame for bloody UFC 189 title challenge |url=https://www.stalbertgazette.com/indigenous/canadas-rory-macdonald-headed-to-ufc-hall-of-fame-for-bloody-ufc-189-title-challenge-6829305 |access-date=2026-07-14 |website=St. Albert Gazette |language=en}} At the end of the fourth round, both fighters stared each other down in the middle of the octagon before being ushered back into their corners. After being nearly finished at the end of the third round, Lawler landed a clean punch on MacDonald's already-broken nose in the fifth round, causing MacDonald to collapse in pain and winning by TKO. Going into the fifth, MacDonald was ahead on all three judge's scorecards, meaning that Lawler likely needed to win by finish.{{Cite web |last=Tucker |first=Bryan |date=2015-07-12 |title=Robbie Lawler vs. Rory MacDonald UFC 189 scorecard |url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2015/7/12/8936243/robbie-lawler-vs-rory-macdonald-ufc-189-scorecard |access-date=2026-07-14 |website=MMA Fighting: UFC, Mixed Martial Arts (MMA) News, Results |language=en-US}}\n\nThe fight was awarded Fight of the Night honors, and met with immediate acclaim. MMA journalist Dave Doyle proclaimed it \"an instant classic,\" and speculated that the fight was MMA's equivalent of [[Arturo Gatti#Fight against De La Hoya and trilogy against Micky Ward|Arturo Gotti]] vs. [[Micky Ward#Ward vs. Gatti trilogy|Mickey Ward]].{{Cite web |last=Doyle |first=Dave |date=2015-07-13 |title=UFC Weekend Aftermath: Pondering the price of an instant classic |url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2015/7/13/8942629/ufc-weekend-aftermath-pondering-the-price-of-an-instant-classic |access-date=2026-07-15 |website=MMA Fighting: UFC, Mixed Martial Arts (MMA) News, Results |language=en-US}} Within two years, a fan poll conducted by ESPN voted it the best fight in UFC history, narrowly edging out [[UFC 165|Jones vs. Gustafsson]].{{Cite web |url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/19124239/ufc-robbie-lawler-vs-rory-macdonald-ii-voted-best-fight-ever |access-date=2026-07-14 |website=www.espn.com|title=Lawler-MacDonald II voted best fight in UFC history|last=Okamoto|first=Brett}} Numerous sources regard it as the greatest fight in the history of the promotion, and it was inducted into the [[UFC Hall of Fame#Fight wing|UFC Hall of Fame's Fight wing]] in 2023.{{cite web | title=Sherdog's Top 10: Best MMA Fights of All-Time | website=Sherdog | date=2016-06-16 | url=//www.sherdog.com/news/articles/8/Sherdogs-Top-10-Best-MMA-Fights-of-AllTime-106475 | access-date=2023-04-25}}{{cite web | title=The Greatest MMA Fights of All Time | website=Tapology | date=2020-12-02 | url=https://www.tapology.com/rankings/all-time-greatest-fights-in-mma-and-ufc-history | access-date=2023-04-25}}{{cite web | title=8 Best Fights In UFC History | website=BetMGM | date=2022-09-09 | url=https://sports.betmgm.com/en/blog/ufc/8-best-fights-in-ufc-history-bm05/ | access-date=2023-04-25}}\n\n==Milestones==\nThis was the first event to feature the UFC's [[Reebok]] fight kits, which were announced on December 2, 2014 and officially revealed on June 30, 2015.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/06/what-you-need-to-know-about-new-ufc-reebok-fight-kit-uniforms-and-gear|title=What you need to know about new UFC-Reebok 'fight kit' uniforms and gear|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-06-30|access-date=2015-06-30}}\n\nFor the main event, the entrance music was performed live. [[Sinéad O'Connor]] sang \"[[Foggy Dew (Irish ballad)|Foggy Dew]]\" for McGregor and [[Aaron Lewis (musician)|Aaron Lewis]] and his band played \"[[Country Boy (Aaron Lewis song)|Country Boy]]\" for Mendes.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/07/ufc-189-results-conor-mcgregor-gets-title-with-second-round-tko-of-chad-mendes|title=UFC 189 results: Conor McGregor gets title with second-round TKO of Chad Mendes|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Steven Marrocco|date=2015-07-12|access-date=2015-07-12}}\n\n==See also==\n*[[List of UFC events]]\n*[[2015 in UFC]]\n\n==References==\n{{Reflist}}\n{{UFC event\n|name=UFC on Fox: Dillashaw vs. Barão 2\n|image=UFC_on_FOX_16_pre_sale.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date= {{start date|2015|07|25}}\n|venue= [[United Center]]\n|city= [[Chicago, Illinois]]\n|attendance=11,663{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/07/ufc-on-fox-16-draws-11663-attendance-for-1-2-million-live-gate|title=UFC on FOX 16 draws 11,663 attendance for 190|UFC 190: Rousey vs. Correia]]\n}}\n'''''UFC on Fox: Dillashaw vs. Barão 2''''' (also known as '''''UFC on Fox 16''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held on July 25, 2015, at the [[United Center]] in [[Chicago, Illinois]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/01/ufc-on-fox-16-official-for-july-25-at-chicagos-united-center|title=UFC on FOX 16 official for July 25 at Chicago's United Center|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-01-14}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event was headlined by a [[UFC Bantamweight Championship]] rematch between current champion [[T.J. Dillashaw]] and former champion [[Renan Barão]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/Dillashaw-Barao-II-Highlights-Chicago-FOX-Card|title=Dillashaw-Barao II highlights Chicago Fox card|publisher=ufc.com|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=2015-04-14|access-date=2015-04-14}}{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/07/lineup-set-for-ufc-on-fox-16-in-chicago-with-eight-bouts-including-title-fight-on-fox|title=Lineup set for UFC on FOX 16 in Chicago with eight bouts, including title fight, on FOX|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-07-03|access-date=2015-07-03}} Their first fight took place at [[UFC 173]] which ended in a fifth round technical knockout victory for Dillashaw that won him the title.{{cite news|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2014/5/25/5749004/ufc-173-results-t-j-dillashaw-wins-bantamweight-title-in-stunning|title=UFC 173 results: T.J. Dillashaw wins bantamweight title in stunning upset|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Dave Doyle|date=2014-05-25|access-date=2015-04-14}} Prior to this event, the rematch had been rescheduled for two other events. The first of which was [[UFC 177]], where Barão withdrew from the bout on the day of weigh-ins, as he had to be admitted to a hospital as a result of his attempts to cut weight.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2014/08/renan-barao-out-of-ufc-177-joe-soto-meets-t-j-dillashaw-for-title|title=Renan Barao out of UFC 177, Joe Soto meets T.J. Dillashaw for title|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Matt Erickson and John Morgan|date=2014-08-29|access-date=2015-04-14}} The second was [[UFC 186]], where Dillashaw withdrew from the bout due to a broken rib.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/03/champ-t-j-dillashaw-injured-title-rematch-with-renan-barao-scrapped-from-ufc-186|title=Champ T.J. Dillashaw injured, title rematch with Renan Barao scrapped from UFC 186|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Matt Erickson|date=2015-03-24|access-date=2015-04-14}}\n\nFormer [[List of UFC champions#Lightweight Championship|UFC Lightweight champion]] [[Anthony Pettis]] was expected to face [[Myles Jury]] at the event. However, Pettis pulled out of the bout on May 7 due to an elbow injury and was replaced by [[Edson Barboza]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/05/anthony-pettis-injured-edson-barboza-steps-in-vs-myles-jury-at-ufc-on-fox-16|title=Anthony Pettis injured, Edson Barboza steps in vs. Myles Jury at UFC on FOX 16|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Mike Bohn|date=2015-05-08|access-date=2015-05-08}} In turn, Jury pulled out the bout on June 9 due to an injury and was replaced by [[Paul Felder]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2015/6/9/8752819/myles-jury-out-paul-felder-in-against-edson-barboza-at-ufc-on-fox-16|title=Myles Jury out, Paul Felder in against Edson Barboza at UFC on FOX 16|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Shaun Al-Shatti|date=2015-06-09|access-date=2015-06-09}}\n\nA lightweight bout between [[Danny Castillo]] and [[Rustam Khabilov]] that was previously scheduled for [[UFC 182]] was expected to take place at this event. However, once again, Khabilov was pulled from the bout due to complications with his [[Visa (document)|visa]]. Castillo remained on the card against [[Jim Miller (fighter)|Jim Miller]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2015/6/22/8825287/rustam-khabilov-out-with-visa-issues-jim-miller-to-face-danny|title=Rustam Khabilov out with visa issues, Jim Miller to face Danny Castillo at UFC on FOX 16|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Marc Raimondi|date=2015-06-21|access-date=2015-06-21}}\n\n[[Erik Koch]] was expected to face [[Ramsey Nijem]] at the event. However, Koch pulled out of the bout in late June due to an injury and was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Andrew Holbrook]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/Holbrook-in-for-Injured-Koch-vs-Nijem|title=Holbrook in for injured Koch vs. Nijem |publisher=ufc.com|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=2015-06-29|access-date=2015-06-29}}\n\n[[Antônio Braga Neto]] was expected to face [[Zak Cummings]] at this event. However, Neto pulled out of the bout in early July due to an injury and was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Dominique Steele]].{{cite web|url=http://www.foxsports.com/ufc/story/zak-cummings-gets-new-opponent-for-fight-night-chicago-070815|title=Zak Cummings gets new opponent for Fight Night Chicago|publisher=foxsports.com|author=Johnathan Bradley|date=2015-07-08|access-date=2015-07-08}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (Fox)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Bantamweight\n|[[T.J. web|url=http://www.ufc.com/event/ufc-fight-night-chicago-2015|title=UFC on Fox: Dillashaw vs. Barão 2|publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship|access-date=2015-04-14}}\n\n==Bonus awards==\nThe following fighters were awarded $50,000 bonuses:{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/07/ufc-on-fox-16-bonuses-of-course-edson-barboza-vs-paul-felder-got-fight-of-the-night|title=UFC on FOX 16 bonuses: Of course Edson Barboza vs. Paul Felder got 'Fight of the Night'|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-07-25|access-date=2015-07-25}}\n*'''Fight of the Night: Edson Barboza vs. Paul Felder'''\n*'''Performance of the Night: T.J. Dillashaw''' and '''Tom Lawlor'''\n\n==See also==\n*[[List of UFC events]]\n*[[2015 in UFC]]\n\n==References==\n{{Reflist}}\n{{UFC Events}}\n\n{{DEFAULTSORT:UFC on Fox: Dillashaw vs. Barao 2}}\n[[Category:Fox UFC]]\n[[Category:Events at United Center]]\n[[Category:Mixed martial arts events in Chicago]]\n[[Category:2015 in mixed martial arts]]\n[[Category:July 2015 sports events in the United States]]"}}}],"langlinks":[{"lang":"ja","title":"UFC on FOX 16"}]},{"pageid":45539065,"ns":0,"title":"The Ultimate Fighter: American Top Team vs. Blackzilians","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{Short description|US television program}}\n{{Use American English|date=March 2026}}\n{{Use mdy dates|date=September 2023}}\n{{Infobox television\n|image = \n|genre = [[reality television|Reality]], [[Broadcasting of sports events|Sports]]\n|runtime = 60 minutes\n|creator = [[Frank Fertitta III]], [[Lorenzo Fertitta]], [[Dana White]]\n|starring = Dana White, [[American Top Team]], [[Blackzilians]]\n|country = United States\n|network = [[Fox Sports 1]]\n|first_aired = {{Start date|2015|04|22}}\n|last_aired = {{End date|2015|07|08}}\n}}\n\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: American Top Team vs. Blackzilians''''' (also known as '''''The Ultimate Fighter 21''''') was the twenty-first installment of the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC)-produced [[reality television]] series ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''. This season was the first to feature a gym vs. gym theme.{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.com/2015/02/tuf-21-features-att-vs-blackzilians-500k-grand-prize-16-welterweight-cast-members|title='TUF 21' features ATT vs. Blackzilians, $500K grand prize, 16 welterweight cast members|date=February 28, 2015|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|access-date=February 28, 2015}}\n\nThe series was officially announced by the UFC on February 28, 2015. For the first time ever, the UFC reality series featured a format pitting two of the sport's top gyms – [[American Top Team]] and the [[Blackzilians]] – against one another. The season was filmed in [[South Florida]], where the two camps sit just miles apart, and the competitors were welterweights from each facility. An amount of $500,000 was earned throughout the season based on wins and losses for the winning gym.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2015/2/28/8126727/tuf-21-features-american-top-team-vs-blackzilians-with-500k-on-the|title=TUF 21 features American Top Team vs. Blackzilians with $500K on the line|date=February 28, 2015|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Marc Raimondi|access-date=February 28, 2015}}\n\nDan Lambert heads the ATT team, and rival Glenn Robinson heads the Blackzilians club. The series prominently features the rivalry between the two camps, which developed in 2011 after a band of ATT fighters defected and started their own camp with Robinson, who is the gym's owner but not their head coach. The two gyms are located just 20 minutes apart. The teams were made up of eight up-and-coming welterweights from each gym. The 16 men shared a house in the Miami/Fort Lauderdale area but instead of training amongst each other, the athletes still worked with their coaches at their respective gyms.\nThe actual fights took place at American Top Team in [[Coconut Creek, Florida|Coconut Creek]] and the Blackzilians' Jaco Hybrid Training Center in [[Boca Raton, Florida|Boca Raton]]. The winning fighter earned home gym control for his team for the next bout. Opponents were not revealed until weigh-ins for the first time.{{cite web|url=http://www.foxsports.com/watch/the-ultimate-fighter/story/the-ultimate-fighter-american-top-team-vs-blackzilians-debuts-april-22-022815|title='The Ultimate Fighter: American Top Team vs. Blackzilians' debuts April 22|date=February 28, 2015|publisher=foxsports.com|author=Damon Martin|access-date=February 28, 2015}}\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Teams===\n{{Col-begin}}\n{{Col-break}}\n;{{color box|#1034A6|border=silver}} {{color box|#E62020|border=silver}} American Top Team:\n*Dan Lambert, Gym Owner\n*Ricardo Liborio\n*Marcus \"Conan\" Silveira\n*[[Mike Brown (fighter)|Mike Brown]]\n*[[Din Thomas]]\n*Brian Harris\n*[[Steve Bruno]]\n*[[Robbie Lawler]]\n{{Col-break}}\n;{{color box|black|border=silver}} {{color box|#9C9599|border=silver}} Blackzilians:\n*Glenn Robinson, Gym Owner\n*[[Jorge Santiago]]\n*Greg Jones\n*[[Rashad Evans]]\n*[[Tyrone Spong]]\n*André Benkei\n*Corey Peacock\n*Douglas Kalman\n*Jason Gochez\n*[[Michael Johnson (fighter)|Michael Johnson]]\n{{col-end}}\n\n===Fighters===\n*'''American Top Team'''\n**Marcelo Alfaya, [[Steve Carl]], [[Nathan Coy]], [[Michael Graves (fighter)|Michael Graves]], [[Hayder Hassan]], [[Sabah Homasi]], Uros Jurisic, Steve Montgomery.\n''*Montgomery was removed from the competition on episode 4 due to medical reasons. He was replaced by [[Cristiano Souza]] on episode 7.''\n\n*'''Blackzilians'''\n**Valdir Araújo, Carrington Banks, [[Luiz Firmino]], [[Jason Jackson (fighter)|Jason Jackson]], [[Vicente Luque]], Andrews Nakahara, Felipe Portela, [[Kamaru Usman]].\n''*Nakahara was forced out of the competition on episode 7 due to a medical suspension. He was replaced by Alexandre Pimentel.''\n\n==Episodes==\n;'''Episode 1: Rumble in South Florida (April 22, 2015)'''\n*For the first time ever ''The Ultimate Fighter'' will be contested outside of [[Las Vegas]], this time at [[South Florida]]. Both gyms, [[American Top Team]] and [[Blackzilians]], are introduced, as well as their owners. They both share their stories about the inception of their gyms, including Blackzilians formation, as some of ATT fighters back then defected to the soon-to-be formed gym.\n*[[Dana White]] comments on the fact that each owner will choose one fighter but their opponents will not be revealed until the weigh ins. There will be 12 fights in the first round and a fighter can fight up to three times or not at all. He also explains the format of the team competition this season: the first 4 fights will be worth 25 points each, the next 4 fights 50 points each and the final 4 fights will be worth 100 points each. The team with the most points at the end of the 12 fights will earn $200,000. After that, they move to the finale. For a fighter to be eligible, he must have fought two times during the regular season (12 fights). The winning fighter gets $300,000 for their gym.\n*Each gym presents their fighter picks for the tournament.\n*The teams arrive at the mansion in boats and are later greeted by White. He flips a coin (blue for ATT, black for the Blackzilians) of each team to determine the gym that will host the first fight, reminding them that the winner of each fight gets the home gym advantage for the next one. The Blackzilians win the coin toss.\n*Dan Lambert explains to ATT's fighters all the aspects of the season.\n*Glenn Robinson hosts the Blackzilians at his house as they watch their teammate, [[Anthony Kewoa Johnson|Anthony \"Rumble\" Johnson]], defeat [[Alexander Gustafsson]] at the main event of [[UFC on Fox: Gustafsson vs. Johnson]].\n*ATT picks Michael Graves for their first fight. The Blackzilians picks Kamarudeen Usman. Later, both fighters make weight successfully.\n*'''Kamarudeen Usman''' defeated Michael Graves via majority decision (20–18, 20–18, 19–19) after two rounds.\n*After the fight, Hayder Hassan challenged Usman to a fight.\n\n;'''Episode 2: Battle Lines Drawn (April 29, 2015)'''\n*Michael Graves expresses his sour feeling due to his loss on the last and also his opinion that he gave an opportunity for the Blackzilians to gain confidence. His teammates talk about the fact that they have all faced losses before and Graves never has. Due to that, Steve Carl believes his teammate will now have less pressure on his shoulders. Graves is supported by Nathan Coy and Hayder Hassan, who both say that they are all motivated to defeat their opponents.\n*Hassan tries to work mind games on Kamarudeen Usman, but he does not want to take part in it. Later, both fighters comment on the situation with their teammates.\n*After analyzing several options, ATT picks Uros Jurisic. The Blackzilians also consider many options, ultimately picking Luiz \"Buscapé\" Firmino.\n*During a training session at the Fighter's House gym, Usman gets frustrated and angry at Jason Jackson. He leaves the session showing off his anger, but later calms down and apologizes to Jackson.\n*Both fighters make weight and after that, Dan Lambert and Glenn Robinson exchange heated words during a confrontation.\n*'''Luiz Firmino''' defeated Uros Jurisic via unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n\n;'''Episode 3: Settling the Score (May 6, 2015)'''\n*Valdir \"BBMonstro\" Araújo complains about a stolen bottle of [[red wine]]. He believes that somebody from ATT stole it, but all of them deny it. In turn, Michael Graves confirms to Nathan Coy in a [[poker]] match that he drank the wine earlier. Steve Carl comments that his teammate has been doing deliberate things ever since he lost the first fight.\n*After analyzing their options, ATT believes its time to change the game using their veterans and they pick Carl, a former [[List of WSOF champions#Welterweight Championship|WSOF Welterweight champion]].\n*The Blackzilians analyze their training and recovery to decide their next pick. After considering those aspects and his experience, they pick Araújo, who has formerly fought UFC middleweight champion [[Chris Weidman]].\n*Sabah Homasi talks to Steve Montgomery about Araújo's accusations days earlier and Graves' unadmitted guilt over drinking the wine. They both believe that Graves is being weak at the moment and that he needs to pull himself up.\n*During weigh-ins, Carl weighs in at 171.75 lb on his first attempt and must make 171 within one hour. He goes to the Blackzilians' locker room to use their sauna and [[Michael Johnson (fighter)|Michael Johnson]] does not like the fact that they are helping their rival. [[Tyrone Spong]] did not know that and does not like it as well. He discusses the issue with the athletic commission representative and later enters the locker room to confront ATT.\n*Spong and Johnson demand that Carl and ATT leave the locker room. Dan Lambert and Coy argue with them about letting Carl stay as they would do the same if it was the opposite case. Spong says there are no rules about that and Coy mentions that \"no rules\" means that they will stay until their teammate cuts the remaining weight, which leads to a heated confrontation between the two of them. In the end, Carl weighs in at 170 lb.\n*'''Valdir Araújo''' defeated Steve Carl via submission (guillotine choke) in the second round.\n\n;'''Episode 4: Boiling Point (May 13, 2015)'''\n*Steve Carl says that he felt disconnected in the fight, something that \"never happened before.\" Dan Lambert notes that Carl was the last guy he expected to hear that from.\n*Conan Silveira tries to instigate motivation in the fighters and Steve Montgomery links his motivation and fighting spirit to that, citing that he is unafraid of losing, and that it all adds up to his own \"fire.\" The coaches believe that is the right attitude for the team. Later, Montgomery is picked to fight next.\n*Glenn Robinson and the rest of Blackzilians' coaching staff visit their fighters at the house. They speculate on Nathan Coy being their possible foe and [[Jorge Santiago]] believes that Carrington Banks would be a good choice to face him, something that Robinson is unsure about. Despite that, they all reach consensus on picking Banks for the fight, even though he has less experience than most fighters and has not fought in a while.\n*The calmness in the house is suddenly broken by a scream asking for help on the second floor. It turns out that Montgomery is having a [[Tonic–clonic seizure|seizure]], which scares all of his teammates while they try to help him. Emergency is called and they transfer him to a hospital for better evaluation.\n*Lambert and Silveira try to figure out what to do in case Montgomery is not able to fight. They believe their opponent is either Banks or Jason Jackson. They speculate on whether picking Nathan Coy or Sabah Homasi, ultimately picking the latter.\n*Homasi comments that besides fighting, he is also an \"[[stripper|entertainer for women]].\"\n*Montgomery comes back from the hospital accompanied by Lambert. He comments that his seizure was caused by drinking too much water without ingesting any [[electrolytes]] with it. Despite having no [[sequela]]e, he is forced to leave the competition. He notes that he is more disappointed on what he has done to his team than what he did to himself. White then promises him a shot in the UFC if he stays healthy.\n*'''Carrington Banks''' defeated Sabah Homasi via decision after three rounds.\n*Homasi and his coaches are all shocked at the result. Lambert notes that while he was disappointed with some of the previous performances, that was not the case of Homasi.\n\n;'''Episode 5: Battle Wounds (May 20, 2015)'''\n*'''Hayder Hassan''' defeated Andrews Nakahara via TKO (punches) in the first round at 48 seconds.\n*This earns ATT their first win and 50 points, as they have now gained home gym advantage.\n\n;'''Episode 6: Fight Through the Pain (May 27, 2015)'''\n*'''Jason Jackson''' defeated Marcelo Alfaya via majority decision (19–19, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n*Dan Lambert and the ATT coaching staff express frustration with Alfaya, especially due to his major experience. Lambert says that after the fight, Alfaya told him he was saving energy in the first round. That statement made Lambert wish it was said before the fight, as they would have kept him from fighting next.\n*Dana White reveals another change to the season. On the next episode, both ATT and the Blackzilians will have the opportunity to drop two fighters from the team and substitute two alternates.\n\n;'''Episode 7: Eyes on the Prize (June 3, 2015)'''\n*The ATT coaches are still disappointed at their fighters. Conan Silveira urges them to seize the opportunity and perform better. He believes that they are wasting a great chance with poor performances. He also tells them to listen more to the coaches.\n*'''Vicente Luque''' defeated Nathan Coy via submission (anaconda choke) at 2:26 in the third round.\n*Coy was tearful and dejected about the loss, as the Blackzilians now lead 200 to 50 points and retain home gym advantage.\n\n;'''Episode 8: Lightning Strikes Twice (June 10, 2015)'''\n*'''Hayder Hassan''' defeated Felipe Portela via majority decision (20–18, 19–19, 20–18) after two rounds.\n*With the win, Hassan earns ATT a significant 50 points in the competition and takes home gym advantage for the next fight. The Blackzilians still lead 200 points to 100, but with the next four fights rewarding 100 points each, there is potential for ATT to make a comeback and possibly even win.\n\n;'''Episode 9: No Guts, No Glory (June 17, 2015)'''\n*'''Kamarudeen Usman''' defeated Steve Carl via unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n*Carl says that both of his fight during the tournament were the worst of his career and that he does not know whether it is the atmosphere of the reality show or the pressure of letting his team down that made him perform poorly.\n*In order to win the $200,000 team prize, ATT must win three straight fights to end the season.\n\n;'''Episode 10: Do or Die (June 24, 2015)'''\n*'''Nathan Coy''' defeated Valdir Araújo via unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n*Blackzilians still lead 300 to 200 in points with two 100-point fights remaining. However, home gym advantage goes back to ATT for the next fight.\n\n;'''Episode 11: Pushing the Limits (July 1, 2015)'''\n*The celebration in ATT's locker room is huge as there is still a chance of winning the competition. Ricardo Liborio comments that with big adversities, come big opportunities and that's their motto to finish the season strong.\n*Meanwhile, in the Blackzilians' locker room, Glenn Robinson says he is proud of Araújo, but he rants on the fact that his team has no right to celebrate until it's all over and they win the show. Some of the fighters, like Usman and Jackson, believe that some of their teammates have lost the hunger. They felt some of those fighters took the tournament for granted and that's what led to the heated discussion.\n*The Blackzilians have no problem selecting Jason Jackson as their fighter for the next bout. Jackson was the first fighter from their team to win a fight in ATT's gym, when he defeated Marcelo Alfaya on episode 6. He feels confident and ready to repeat the performance.\n*On the opposite site, American Top Team has a bit of an analyzing before picking their fighter. Right after the bat, Cristiano \"Soldado\" Souza is suggested by one of the coaches, while others contemplate bringing Michael Graves in. Graves is highly touted by the coaches, but after losing the first fight of the tournament, he went downhill as he started drinking and losing focus on training and the competition. Despite that, four of the five coaches in the room opt to choose Graves.\n*Both teams visit the Joseph C. Carter Park in [[Fort Lauderdale, Florida|Fort Lauderdale]] to support the \"Owner's Challenge\". It will be a [[relay race]] and the winner will get $10,000 for him and $1,500 for each of his team's fighters. ATT will feature coach Brian Harris, Sabah Homasi, Cristiano Souza and Dan Lambert, while Blackzilians will feature Carrington Banks, coach Greg Jones, Jason Jackson and Glenn Robison. After a very close race, Lambert gets the win for ATT.\n*In the kitchen, Usman confronts Homasi over comments about him holding the cage in previous fight, which leads to a heated argument.\n*Before the fight, Graves appears to have injured his neck but he is still going to fight.\n*'''Michael Graves''' defeated Jason Jackson via submission (rear-naked choke) at 3:01 in the first round.\n*After the fight, an infuriated Robinson complains that Graves wasn't defending himself after Jackson landed a kick (apparently to the groin) and the fight should have been stopped. He only calms down after Liborio talks to him and reminds him that it's not worth to let the situation escalate to a brawl between the teams. Outside of the gym, Robinson confronts the referee and the commissioner with the same complaints.\n*The performance replay confirms that while Jackson's foot hit Graves' body, his shin indeed hit Grave's groin. Dana White notes that if anyone should complain about the referee, it is the American Top Team, not Robinson.\n*The result brings the competition even as both teams have 300 points. ATT retains home gym advantage for the final bout of the season.\n\n;'''Episode 12: Showdown (July 8, 2015)'''\n*Glenn Robinson reminds the Blackzilians that they were proving themselves as the better team until they became overconfident and started losing. He asks the fighters if they want to be reminded as a team that started strong and finished weak. Right off the bat, Robinson announces Vicente Luque as their final pick of the season.\n*Meanwhile, ATT discusses their next pick at the house. Dan Lambert asks Hayder Hassan who would he pick if he was the coach. Hassan wastes no time replying \"me\" and everyone agrees that he should be the one to finish it off for them. Lambert states that it is because of Hassan that they are still on the competition. Hassan will be the only fighter to fight three times this season.\n*'''Hayder Hassan''' defeated Vicente Luque via split decision (29–28, 28–29, 29–28) after three rounds.\n*Madness takes over the American Top Team as they celebrate their enormous comeback to win the competition 400 to 300 points and also get $200,000.\n*As celebration takes place in ATT's locker room, desolation is in the air at the Blackzilians. They praise Luque's effort as well as the whole team.\n*Both teams will close the season with their picks for the live finale on July 12 at the [[MGM Grand Garden Arena]] in [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], worth a [[Harley Davidson]] motorcycle, $300,000 and the TUF trophy. ATT can choose between Michael Graves, Nathan Coy and Hassan. While they believe Graves would be a tough match up against anyone from their opponents, they all agree that Hassan is their man, as he brought half of ATT's points. Blackzilians also has doubts as some coaches want to pick Luque again, while Rashad Evans voices his opinion that Kamarudeen Usman should be their guy due to his performances and two victories.\n*Both teams gather at ATT's gym, while Dana White participates via video conference. Before the selections, Lambert announces that the whole team decided to donate the $200,000 prize to the [[Wounded Warrior Project]], a charity and veterans service organization that offers a variety of programs, service and events for wounded veterans of the military actions following [[September 11 attacks|the events of September 11, 2001]].\n*ATT and Blackzilians announce their fighters for the finale: Hayder Hassan vs. Kamarudeen Usman.\n\n==Tournament==\n\n===Results table===\n{| class=\"wikitable sortable\" style=\"font-size:85%; text-align:left; width:30%;\"\n|-\n!Teams\n!Total points\n!Wins\n|-\n|{{color box|#1034A6|border=silver}} '''American Top Team'''\n|400\n|5\n|-\n|{{color box|black|border=silver}} Blackzilians\n|300\n|7\n|}\n\n==The Ultimate Fighter 21 Finale==\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=The Ultimate Fighter: American Top Team vs. Blackzilians Finale\n|image=TUF 21 Finale pre sale.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date={{Start date|2015|07|12}}\n|venue=[[MGM Grand Garden Arena]]\n|city= [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], [[Nevada]]\n|attendance=4,844{{cite news|url=https://mmajunkie.com/2015/07/tuf-21-finale-draws-reported-4844-attendance-for-291000-live-gate|title=TUF 21 Finale draws reported 4,844 attendance for $291,000 live gate|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-07-13|access-date=2015-07-13}}\n|gate=$291,000\n|previousevent=[[UFC 189|UFC 189: Mendes vs. McGregor]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Mir vs. Duffee]]\n}}\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: American Top Team vs. Blackzilians Finale''''' (also known as '''''The Ultimate Fighter 21 Finale''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held on July 12, 2015, at the [[MGM Grand Garden Arena]] in [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], Nevada.{{cite web|url=https://www.ufc.com/news/Summer-UFC-Events-Secured-for-Florida-and-Nevada|title=Summer UFC Events Secured for Florida and Nevada|publisher=ufc.com|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=2015-05-15|access-date=2015-05-15}}\n\n===Background===\nThe event was originally expected to be held in [[South Florida]].{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2015/4/15/8421113/benson-henderson-vs-michael-johnson-targeted-for-tuf-21-finale-in|title=Benson Henderson vs. Michael Johnson targeted for TUF 21 Finale in Florida; venue to be determined|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2015-04-15|access-date=2015-04-16}} However, on May 15, the event was moved to the [[MGM Grand Garden Arena]] in [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], Nevada.\n\nA lightweight bout between former [[List of World Extreme Cagefighting champions#Lightweight Championship|WEC]] & [[UFC Lightweight Championship|UFC Lightweight champion]] [[Benson Henderson]] and [[Michael Johnson (fighter)|Michael Johnson]] was initially linked as the event headliner. However, on May 15, a welterweight bout between [[Jake Ellenberger]] and [[Stephen Thompson (fighter)|Stephen Thompson]], originally booked for a day earlier at [[UFC 189]], was announced as the main event. Although never officially announced by the UFC, the bout between Henderson and Johnson did not take place at this event.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2015/5/15/8612141/benson-henderson-vs-michael-johnson-off-tuf-21-finale-card|title=Benson Henderson vs. Michael Johnson off TUF 21 Finale card|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2015-05-15|access-date=2015-05-15}}\n\nThe final of ''The Ultimate Fighter: American Top Team vs. Blackzilians'' took place at the event.\n\nMárcio Alexandre Jr. was scheduled to face [[George Sullivan (fighter)|George Sullivan]] at the event. However, Alexandre pulled out of the fight during the week leading up to the event citing a rib injury and was replaced by promotional newcomer Dominic Waters.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2015/7/5/8895639/marcio-alexandre-jr-out-with-rib-injury-george-sullivan-now-meets|title=Marcio Alexandre Jr. out with rib injury, George Sullivan now meets Dominic Waters at TUF 21 Finale|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Guilherme Cruz|date=2015-07-05|access-date=2015-07-05}}\n\n[[Maximo Blanco]] missed weight on his first attempt at the weigh-ins, coming in at 148.5 lb. After having made no attempts to cut further, he was fined 20 percent of his fight purse, which went to [[Mike De La Torre]].{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.com/2015/07/the-ultimate-fighter-21-finale-live-and-official-weigh-in-results|title=TUF 21 Finale weigh-in results: Headliners, 'TUF' finalists hit their marks; two miss|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-07-11|access-date=2015-07-11}}\n\nDuring the live broadcast, it was announced that the next season of The Ultimate Fighter would be coached by current interim [[UFC Featherweight Championship|UFC Featherweight Champion]] [[Conor McGregor]], who will lead a team of 8 European lightweights and former [[WEC Featherweight Championship|WEC Featherweight Champion]] [[Urijah Faber]] will coach a team of 8 American lightweights. They did not fight each other at the end of the season.{{cite web|url=https://www.ufc.com/news/McGregor-Faber-to-Coach-The-Ultimate-Fighter|title=McGregor, Faber to coach TUF|publisher=ufc.com|author=Staff|date=2015-07-12|access-date=2015-07-12}}\n\n===Results===\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (Fox Sports 1)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Stephen Vicente Luque: $10,000 \n* '''Jorge Masvidal:''' $102,000 (includes $51,000 win bonus) def. Cezar Ferreira: $20,000\n* '''Michelle Waterson:''' $30,000 (includes $15,000 win bonus) def. Angela Magaña: $10,000\n* '''Maximo Blanco:''' $41,400 (includes $23,000 win bonus) def. Mike De La Torre: $17,600 ^\n* '''Josh Samman:''' $20,000 (includes $10,000 win bonus) def. Darrell Montague: $10,000\n\n^ ''Maximo Blanco was fined 20 percent of his purse ($5,600) for failing to make the required weight for his fight with Mike De La Torre. on Fox: Dillashaw vs. Barão 2]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Teixeira vs. Saint Preux]]\n}}\n'''''UFC 190: Rousey vs. Correia''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held on August 1, 2015, at the [[Jeunesse Arena|HSBC Arena]] in [[Rio de Janeiro, Brazil]].{{cite news|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/Rousey-Correia-Headline-190-Rio|title=Rousey headlines UFC 190 in Rio against Correia|publisher=ufc.com|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=2015-03-20|access-date=2015-03-20}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event was headlined by a [[UFC Women's Bantamweight Championship]] bout between former UFC Bantamweight champion [[Ronda Rousey]] and #5 contender [[Bethe Correia]].\n\nThe lightweight and bantamweight finals of ''[[The Ultimate Fighter: Brazil 4]]'' also took place at this event.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2015/5/15/8610043/lyoto-machida-vs-yoel-romero-set-as-new-main-event-for-tuf-brazil-4|title=Lyoto Machida vs. Yoel Romero set as new main event for TUF Brazil: 4 Finale in Miami|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Guilherme Cruz|date=2015-05-15|access-date=2015-05-15}} The finale was initially planned to take place on June 27, 2015 in [[São Paulo|São Paulo, Brazil]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2015/4/14/8417343/tuf-brazil-4-finale-set-for-june-27-in-sao-paulo|title=TUF: Brazil 4 Finale set for June 27 in Sao Paulo|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Guilherme Cruz|date=2015-04-14|access-date=2015-04-16}} However, on May 15, [[UFC Fight Night 70|the event]] was moved to the [[Seminole Hard Rock Hotel and Casino Hollywood|Seminole Hard Rock Hotel and Casino]] in [[Hollywood, Florida]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/Summer-UFC-Events-Secured-for-Florida-and-Nevada|title=Summer UFC Events Secured for Florida and Nevada|publisher=ufc.com|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=2015-05-15|access-date=2015-05-15}} It was expected to be the first time an Ultimate Fighter Brazil Finale would take place outside of Brazil. However, as the event approached, several international fighters experienced potential travel restrictions due to technical issues within the [[Bureau of Consular Affairs]] division of the [[U.S. State Department]] which produces [[visa (document)|visas]]. This led to a situation in which several participants might have been barred from being able to compete.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/06/visa-issues-could-force-several-brazilian-fighters-out-of-ufc-fight-night-70|title=Visa issues could force several Brazilian fighters out of UFC Fight Night 70|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Mike Bohn|date=2015-06-18|access-date=2015-06-18}} Subsequently, the divisional finals of ''The Ultimate Fighter: Brazil 4'' were shifted to this event.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2015/6/19/8811849/visa-issues-forces-changes-at-ufc-fight-night-70-ufc-190|title=Visa issues forces changes at UFC Fight Night 70, UFC 190|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Guilherme Cruz|date=2015-06-19|access-date=2015-06-19}}{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2015/6/22/8823741/ufc-190-card-complete-with-addition-of-tuf-brazil-4-bantamweight|title=UFC 190 card complete with addition of TUF Brazil 4 bantamweight, lightweight finals|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Guilherme Cruz|date=2015-06-22|access-date=2015-06-22}}\n\nThe event featured a potential [[UFC Women's Strawweight Championship]] title eliminator bout between [[Cláudia Gadelha]] and former [[WSOF Women's Strawweight Championship|WSOF Women's Strawweight champion]] [[Jessica Aguilar]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/06/womens-mma-report-joanna-jedrzejczyk-keeps-ufc-strawweight-belt|title=Women's MMA Report: Joanna Jedrzejczyk keeps UFC strawweight belt|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Robert Sargent|date=2015-06-29|access-date=2015-06-29}}\n\nThe event fared well, receiving 900,000 pay-per-view buys.\n\nRousey dedicated her fight to \n[[Roddy Piper|\"Rowdy\" Roddy Piper]] who had died the day before.{{Cite web|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2015/8/28/9219717/ronda-rousey-nearly-breaks-one-million-pay-per-view-buys-with-ufc-190-bethe-correia-mma|title=Rousey Pulls over 900k PPV Buys for UFC 190|date=28 August 2015}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Women's Bantamweight\n|[[Ronda Rousey]] (c)\n|def.\n|[[Bethe Correia]]\n|KO (punch)\n|1\n|0:34\n|For the news|url=http://www.foxsports.com/ufc/story/frank-mir-vs-todd-duffee-will-main-event-ufc-fight-night-in-san-diego-040815|title=Frank Mir vs. Todd Duffee will main event UFC Fight Night in San Diego|work=foxsports.com|author=Damon Martin|date=2015-04-08|access-date=2015-04-08}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event was headlined by a heavyweight bout between former [[UFC Heavyweight Championship|UFC Heavyweight champion]] [[Frank Mir]] and touted prospect [[Todd Duffee]].\n\n[[Bobby Green (fighter)|Bobby Green]] was expected to face [[Al Iaquinta]] at the event. However, Green pulled out of the fight on June 17 due to an ACL tear and was replaced by former [[List of World Extreme Cagefighting champions#Lightweight Championship|WEC]] and [[List of Strikeforce champions#Lightweight Championship|Strikeforce Lightweight champion]] [[Gilbert Melendez]].{{cite news|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2015/6/17/8802075/gilbert-melendez-agrees-to-fight-al-iaquinta-at-ufc-fight-night-san|title=Gilbert Melendez agrees to fight Al Iaquinta at UFC Fight Night San Diego|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2015-06-17|access-date=2015-06-17}} On July 6, it was announced that Melendez tested positive for [[testosterone (medication)|testosterone]] metabolites of an exogenous origin at [[UFC 188]]. He denied using anything, but stated that he is \"responsible for the products he uses and their implications\". Melendez was suspended one year, effective from the date of his last bout. As a result, he was pulled from the fight against Iaquinta, who in turn, was also removed from the card.{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/UFC-Statement-on-Gilbert-Melendez|title=UFC statement on Gilbert Melendez|publisher=ufc.com|author=UFC press release|date=2015-07-06|access-date=2015-07-06}}{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/With-Gilbert-Melendez-Suspended-Al-Iaquinta-Scratched-from-UFC-Fight-Night-in-San-Diego-88657|title=With Gilbert Melendez suspended, Al Iaquinta scratched from UFC Fight Night in San Diego|publisher=sherdog.com|author=Staff|date=2015-07-06|access-date=2015-07-06}}\n\nA featherweight bout between [[Rani Yahya]] and [[Masanori Kanehara]] initially slated for [[UFC Fight Night 70]], was shifted to this event, as several participants on that card were unable to secure [[visa (document)|travel visas]] in time for that event restricting their travel to the United States.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/06/visa-issues-force-massive-shuffling-at-next-weeks-ufc-fight-night-70-event|title=Visa issues force massive shuffling at next week's UFC Fight Night 70 event |publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-06-19|access-date=2015-06-19}}\n\n[[Edgar García (mixed martial artist)|Edgar Garcia]] was expected to face [[Andrew Craig]] at the event. However, Garcia was forced out of the bout with an injury and was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Lyman Good]].{{cite web|url=http://themmareport.com/2015/06/lyman-good-signs-with-ufc-replaces-edgar-garcia-at-ufc-fight-night-71-against-andrew-craig/|title=Lyman Good signs with UFC, replaces Edgar Garcia at UFC Fight Night 71 against Andrew Craig |publisher=themmareport.com|author=Staff|date=2015-06-24|access-date=2015-06-24}}\n\n[[Doo Ho Choi]] was expected to face [[Sam Sicilia]] at the event. However, Choi pulled out of the bout in late June after suffering broken ribs in training and was replaced by [[Yaotzin Meza]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/Choi-Injured-Meza-to-Face-Sicilia|title=Choi Injured, Meza to Face Sicilia|publisher=UFC.com|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=2015-07-01|access-date=2015-07-01}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (Fox Sports 1)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|[[Frank Mir]]\n|def.\n|[[Todd Duffee]]\n|KO Andrew Craig: $17,000\n\n==See also==\n*[[List of UFC events]]\n*[[2015 in UFC]]\n\n==References==\n{{Reflist}}\n{{UFC Events}}\n\n[[Category:UFC Fight Night]]\n[[Category:2015 in mixed martial arts]]\n[[Category:Mixed martial arts in California]]\n[[Category:Mixed martial arts events in San Diego]]\n[[Category:Sports competitions in San Diego]]\n[[Category:July 2015 sports events in the United States]]\n[[Category:2015 in sports in California]]\n[[Category:2015 in San Diego]]\n\n{{mixedmartialart-stub}}"}}}],"langlinks":[{"lang":"ja","title":"UFC Fight Night: Mir vs. Duffee"}]},{"pageid":46492152,"ns":0,"title":"The Ultimate Fighter: Latin America 2","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts television series and event in 2015}}\n{{Infobox television\n|image = \n|genre = [[reality television|Reality]], [[Broadcasting of sports events|Sports]]\n|runtime = 60 minutes\n|creator = [[Frank Fertitta III]], [[Lorenzo Fertitta]], [[Dana White]]\n|starring = Dana White, [[Efraín Escudero]], and [[Kelvin Gastelum]]\n|network = [[Televisa]], UFC Fight Pass\n|first_aired = {{Start date|2015|08|26}}\n|last_aired = {{End date|2015|11|11}}\n}}\n\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: Latin America 2''''' was an installment of the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC)-produced [[reality television]] series ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''. It was the ninth international series and the second to feature mainly Latin American fighters.\n\nOn October 29, 2014, it was announced that the coaches for the season would be [[Kelvin Gastelum]] and [[Efraín Escudero]]. As the coaches compete in two separate weight classes, they are not expected to face each other at the end of the season.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2015/3/4/8146645/kelvin-gastelum-efrain-escudero-to-act-as-coaches-for-tuf-latin|title=Kelvin Gastelum, Efrain Escudero to act as coaches for TUF Latin America 2|date=March 4, 2015|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Marc Raimondi|access-date=March 4, 2015}}\n\nThe UFC held open tryouts for the show on March 29, 2015. The casting call was for [[Lightweight (MMA)|Lightweight]] and [[Welterweight (MMA)|Welterweight]] fighters who are at least 21 years old and have a minimum of two wins in three professional fights.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2015/3/4/8150223/the-ultimate-fighter-22-tryouts-slated-for-lightweights-welterweights|title='The Ultimate Fighter 22' tryouts slated for lightweights, welterweights on March 29|date=March 4, 2015|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Shaun Al-Shatti|access-date=March 4, 2015}} The cast was announced on April 22, 2015.{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/TUF-LATAM-2-Cast-Revealed|title=TUF Latin America 2 Cast Revealed|date=April 22, 2015|publisher=ufc.com|author=Staff|access-date=April 22, 2015}}{{cite web|url=http://www.latintimes.com/meet-complete-cast-ultimate-fighter-latin-america-photos-311723|title=Meet the complete cast of 'The Ultimate Fighter Latin America'|date=April 24, 2015|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Jessica Lucia Roiz|access-date=April 24, 2015}}\n\nThe cast consisted of fighters from 9 countries: Arzamendia, from [[Paraguay]]; Barzola and Olano, from [[Peru]]; Meza and Ramos, from [[Panama]]; Ortega, from [[Chile]]; Soto, from [[Nicaragua]]; Fernández, from [[Honduras]]; Marín, from [[Spain]]; Medinilla, from [[Argentina]] and Gutiérrez, [[Polo Reyes]], Salas, Aldana, Herrera and Montaño, from [[Mexico]].\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Teams===\n{{Col-begin}}\n{{Col-break}}Boxing Coach Wil Beech\n;{{Color box|#00703C|border=silver}} {{Color box|black|border=silver}} Team Gastelum:\n*[[Kelvin Gastelum]], Head Coach\n*Victor Davila, Jiu Jitsu Coach\n*Terrell Hunsinger Jr.\n*Brian Beaumont\n{{Col-break}}\n;{{Color box|#E62020|border=silver}} {{Color box|black|border=silver}} Team Escudero:\n*[[Efraín Escudero]], Head Coach\n*Augusto Mendes, Jiu Jitsu Coach\n*John Crouch, Jiu Jitsu Coach\n*Shaine Jaime, Wrestling Coach\n*Jarret Aki, Strength and Conditioning Coach\n*[[Benson Henderson]]\n*[[Yaotzin Meza]]\n*Wil Beech Boxing Muay Thai Coach{{col-end}}\n\n===Fighters===\n*'''Team Gastelum'''\n**'''Welterweights:''' Erick Montaño, Enrique Marín, [[Hector Aldana]], and Wilmer Fernandez \n**'''Lightweights:''' Cesar Arzamendia, Danny Salas, Jonathan Ortega, and Christhian Soto \n*'''Team Escudero'''\n**'''Welterweights:''' Kevin Medinilla, Marco Olano, [[Álvaro Herrera]], and Vernon Ramos\n**'''Lightweights:''' Horacio Gutiérrez, [[Enrique Barzola]], [[Polo Reyes]], and Oliver Meza\n\n==Episodes==\n;'''Episode 1: (August 26, 2015)'''\n* Unlike the first season from Latin America, the second does not feature region versus region, but instead, Gastelum and Escudero were able to evaluate all the fighters on the show and then select their eight-man teams for the competition this year.\n* Gastelum and Escudero go through their selections with the rest of their coaching staffs to begin picking the teams. Since Escudero won the coin toss, he was allowed to decide whether he would get the first fighter or choose the first fight. Escudero opts to make the first selection and he went with Kevin Medinilla. Gastelum opted for Cesar Arzamendia and the choices went back and forth for 16 selections overall until all the welterweights and lightweights had been broken up into two teams.\n* Once the teams are selected, Gastelum is allowed to make his selection for the first fight and he goes with his No. 1 pick at welterweight, Cesar Arzamendia, against the final pick for Team Escudero, Oliver Meza.\n* The fighters get a surprise when they get a visit by former Ultimate Fighter champion [[Forrest Griffin]]. \n* '''Cesar Arzamendia''' defeated Oliver Meza via first-round TKO (punches).\n* Gastelum announces the first welterweight fight: Erick Montaño vs. Marco Olano.\n\n;'''Episode 2: (September 2, 2015)'''\n* Team Escudero member Horacio Gutiérrez suffered a sprained ankle during a training session, limiting his ability to practice.\n* To mask his injury from the other team, Gutiérrez and his roommates decided to move his bed from the top bunk down to the floor so he could sleep there instead of being forced to jump or climb up on a sprained ankle. In turn, Gutiérrez kept his foot packed in ice hidden underneath the blankets in hopes of keeping his injury a secret. Whether Team Gastelum found out about the injury or Gutiérrez was already targeted, he found out later in the episode that the ankle would be tested.\n*The first welterweight fight of the season was contested between Erick Montaño and Marco Olano. The fight came to an abrupt halt midway through the first round as Olano suffered a fractured arm while landing awkwardly after being taken down by Montaño. \n* '''Erick Montaño''' defeated Marco Olano via first-round TKO (arm injury).\n* Gastelum announces the next lightweight fight: Danny Salas vs. Horacio Gutiérrez.\n\n;'''Episode 3: (September 9, 2015)'''\n* Marco Olano's injury was on the mind of Horacio Gutiérrez after he suffered an ankle injury and then got picked to compete next. Word of his injury had leaked out and he was selected to fight in just a few days. Gutiérrez refused to let an ankle injury knock him out of the fight or the competition although even he admitted it was impossible not to think about his ailment as the bout with Salas drew near.\n* Gutiérrez's coach, Efrain Escudero brought in former Strikeforce women's bantamweight champion fighter Miesha Tate to help with his training while also assisting the rest of the team with some drills. \n* Marco Olano returns from the hospital with his arm in a sling but is not expected to suffer any permanent damage from the injury. He promises to stay at the house and continue to cheer on Team Escudero from the sidelines despite being eliminated from the competition.\n* '''Horacio Gutiérrez''' defeated Danny Salas via decision (majority) after three rounds.\n* Escudero announces the next welterweight fight: Kevin Medinilla vs. Enrique Marín.\n\n;'''Episode 4: (September 16, 2015)'''\n* Fighters from Team Gastelum were upset that Team Escudero had paid up on their bet from a couple of weeks ago. Leading into the fight between Erick Montaño and Marco Olano, Team Escudero and Team Gastelum wagered that the losing side in the bout would be forced to take their mattresses and pillows out to the tennis courts and sleep out under the stars for a night. \n* Team Escudero opts to offer one more bet – double or nothing – and the losing team would be forced to clean the entire house, from top to bottom, while wearing bunny ears. Team Gastelum accepts. \n* '''Enrique Marín''' defeated Kevin Medinilla via decision (majority) after two rounds.\n* Gastelum announces the next lightweight fight: Jonathan Ortega vs. Enrique Barzola.\n\n;'''Episode 5: (September 23, 2015)'''\n* The fighters are paid a visit by former Mexican national soccer coach [[Miguel Herrera|Miguel “El Piojo” Herrera]], and most of the fighters are enthralled when he arrives. Herrera invites the fighters from Team Gastelum and Team Escudero to another competition, playing soccer. \n* The losing side will have to wash the winning team's van that they use to travel from the house to the gym and do it while wearing a thong. Team Gastelum wins the game after a spirited match 2 – 1.\n* Team Escudero carries out their bet and washed the vans, but they decided to do so while clothed. When one of the fighters from Team Gastelum decides to try to make them pay up to the letter of the law, Escudero answers back by carrying the hose into the house and turning the car wash into a water and food fight. \n* '''Enrique Barzola''' defeated Jonathan Ortega via decision (unanimous) after three rounds.\n* Escudero announces the next welterweight fight: Álvaro Herrera vs. [[Hector Aldana]].\n\n;'''Episode 6: (September 30, 2015)'''\n* To get his team ready for the battle ahead, Escudero decides to unveil a new conditioning program that punishes the fighters' bodies to get them ready with superior cardio and conditioning. \n* Back at the house, the fighters were paid a visit from Mexican actress [[Martha Higareda]] who showed up out of nowhere to spend some time with them for a day. The guys were immediately scrambling to pick things up off the floor to make a good impression on the beautiful actress.\n* After the fighters showed Higareda a few of their moves, she offered them a night out on the town with her in Las Vegas. The fighters had been locked up for about four weeks, so they were thrilled, making a night out at a hibachi grill eating some food and having a few drinks being the perfect refresher. \n* '''[[Hector Aldana]]''' defeated Álvaro Herrera via decision (unanimous) after two rounds.\n* Gastelum announces the next lightweight fight: Christihian Soto vs. Marco Polo Reyes.\n\n;'''Episode 7: (October 7, 2015)'''\n* Following the previous loss, the fighters from Team Escudero are convinced that their coach is over training them in the lead up to the competition as the fighters are still struggling with their conditioning after a few brutal weeks of training. They decide to talk to coach Escudero about the training regimen that feels a lot more like breaking them down right now than building them back up again. \n* The fighters get a break and go to a local gym in Las Vegas, where they meet up with Dana White and [[Montserrat Oliver]], who helps introduce this season's coach's challenge. As Escudero and Gastelum step into the gym, White informs the coaches that they will be sumo wrestling with bloated outfits and a simple game where Escudero and Gastelum will battle each other with the winner declared after scoring five points. Despite the weight disparity, Escudero holds his own against Gastelum as the two of them volley back and forth throughout the match. Finally, with the score tied, Escudero is able to trip up Gastelum and send him to the mat and outside the ring to end the match. Team Escudero finally wins a team competition after losing the two previous games this season and everybody on the team will get $1,500 as a prize while the coach leaves with an extra $10,000 to celebrate. \n* [[Polo Reyes]] defeated Christihian Soto via TKO (punches) in the second round.\n* Escudero announces the next welterweight fight: Vernon Ramos vs. Wilmer Fernandez.\n\n;'''Episode 8: (October 14, 2015)'''\n* Panama's Vernon Ramos grew up around violence and in 2012, he lost his father under mysterious circumstances. His father was a big fight fan who wore a UFC hat around the house as he supported his son's dreams to make it to the big show one day. Ramos recalled that his father gave him a big hug and told the young man that from now on he needed to take care of his mother. He asked his father where he was going and he just said “for a walk.” Ramos' father opened the door and closed it behind him and that was the last time he ever saw him again. Rumors followed that he was murdered, and has yet to be solved. Ramos carries his father's hat with him at all times as he competes in The Ultimate Fighter. \n* Ramos' opponent this week is Team Gastelum fighter Wilmer Fernandez, who endured the death of his mother to liver cancer just three days before he left to do The Ultimate Fighter. Much like Ramos, Fernandez is competing in this tournament for a chance at a better life and as a way to honor his fallen parent. \n* '''Vernon Ramos''' defeated Wilmer Fernandez via decision (unanimous) after three rounds.\n* The semifinal match-ups are hand-picked by Dana White:\n:Welterweight fights: Enrique Marín vs. [[Hector Aldana]], and Erick Montaño vs. Vernon Ramos.\n:Lightweight fights: Cesar Arzamendia vs. Enrique Barzola, and Horacio Gutiérrez vs. [[Polo Reyes]].\n* White announces the next welterweight fight: Enrique Marín vs. [[Hector Aldana]].\n\n;'''Episode 9: (October 21, 2015)'''\n* Team Escudero decided to use their down time to get a few more pranks going, and the setup was rather elaborate as they targeted the coaches, including head coach Efrain Escudero. The plot involved putting on a “play” for the coaches at the house, but the fighters opted to make this a much more realistic portrayal of a famous battle when they surprised Escudero and his assistants with a full-on assault, including buckets of ice water and water balloons. For the second time this season, The Ultimate Fighter house got doused in water as the fighters and coaches from Team Escudero chased each other around with balloons and buckets. \n* Following the water fight at the house, it was time for Enrique Marín and [[Hector Aldana]] to get ready for the fight as the two roommates set friendship aside to face off in the Octagon. Marín stated in the lead up to the fight that he felt like he was the better, more technical of the two but he still needed to be composed or risk getting drawn into a brawl with his Mexican opponent. \n* '''Enrique Marín''' defeated [[Hector Aldana]] via submission (rear-naked choke) in the first round.\n* White announces the next lightweight fight: Horacio Gutiérrez vs. [[Polo Reyes]].\n\n;'''Episode 10: (October 28, 2015)'''\n* As this week's fighters, (Team Escudero teammates) Horacio Gutiérrez and Marco Polo Reyes got into the final hours of training before weighing in and facing off, Ultimate Fighter season one winner [[Diego Sanchez]] paid a visit to the gym to meet the competitors from Team Escudero and walk them through a few training techniques. Sanchez is a legend from the first season of the show and he's faced a who's who list of fighters during a UFC career that's spanned the last decade, and this was his chance to give back to the next generation of Ultimate Fighters. Several of the fighters on the show weren't even in high school by the time Sanchez was wrapping up his Ultimate Fighter title, so it was a real treat for them to train with an iconic figure of the mixed martial arts scene. \n* After the visit from Sanchez, the fighters also got the chance to escape the house this week and ride dune buggies all over the desert in Las Vegas. The trip to the desert was the perfect escape for everyone to unwind, although it was clear that both Gutiérrez and Reyes were more focused on what was going down between the two of them in just a matter of hours.\n* '''Horacio Gutiérrez''' defeated [[Polo Reyes]] via TKO (punches) in the first round.\n* White announces the final welterweight semifinal: Erick Montaño vs. Vernon Ramos.\n\n;'''Episode 11: (November 4, 2015)'''\n* The monotony of living in the house with no contact with the outside world has started to make more than a few of the fighters a little stir crazy, and it's impossible for those feelings not to affect both Montaño and Ramos as they get ready for their fight this week. In past seasons, some of the competitors who are the last to perform on the show find that it's tough to get great training or even accessible training partners to help them push the pace for weight cuts and last minute touches before a fight. \n* Former Ultimate Fighter season one winner Diego Sanchez returned, this time lending his support to Team Gastelum. Sanchez was a welcome addition to the training sessions for Escudero's squad and it's the same for Gastelum, as the veteran UFC fighter helped pass along a few tips both inside and outside the cage for the young group of hopefuls that look to join him in the Octagon one day.\n* '''Erick Montaño''' defeated Vernon Ramos via submission (rear-naked choke) in the first round.\n* White announces the final lightweight semifinal: Cesar Arzamendia vs. Enrique Barzola.\n\n;'''Episode 12: (November 11, 2015)'''\n* Former lightweight champion [[Benson Henderson]] stopped by for a day of training at the request of his teammate Efrain Escudero. Henderson is not only one of the best fighters in the world while competing at both 155 and 170 pounds, but he's become a real team leader at his home gym in Arizona, the [[MMA Lab]]. Henderson brought some of those leadership skills to Team Escudero this week while sparring with some of the guys as well. At one point, Henderson was doing some mat work with Álvaro Herrera and he tagged him with a body shot while sparring on the ground. It wasn't the hardest punch in the world and Henderson wasn't going full strength, but the short shot stunned Herrera and left him moaning on the mat, unable to get up for a few minutes. He recovered and continued training, but Henderson's shot showed just how effective a punch to the body can be when it's perfectly placed, no matter how hard it's thrown. Henderson reminded the fighters that learning in the gym is never easy, but those hard lessons will prepare them for the war ahead when they actually step into the Octagon. \n* It was an emotional goodbye for several of the fighters, including Wilmer Fernandez, who was brought to tears as he says a heartfelt goodbye to a group of guys he considers family now. The same can be said for Arzamendia, who thanks everyone for their help getting him ready and for the friendship they've all forged over the last six weeks. Finally, it's Kelvin Gastelum's turn, as the head coach even turns on the waterworks as he prepares his last fighter for battle this season before saying farewell to everybody. The emotional outpouring continues back at the house as well when Escudero and his staff bring over a last dinner for everyone on their team to share. It's filled with a few more laughs than the goodbye meeting at Team Gastelum, but in the end, the sentiment is the same as these eight fighters went from strangers to brothers while living and training together for the last month and a half.\n* '''Enrique Barzola''' defeated Cesar Arzamendia via submission (punch to body) in the first round.\n\n==Tournament Bracket==\n\n===Lightweight Dillashaw vs. Barão 2]]\n}}\n'''''UFC Fight Night: Bisping vs. Leites''''' (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night 72''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held on July 18, 2015 at the [[OVO Hydro]] in [[Glasgow]], [[Scotland]].{{cite news|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/tickets-on-sale-may-29-glasgow|title=Tickets on sale Friday, May 29 for debut Scottish event|publisher=ufc.com|author=UFC Press Release|date=2015-04-26|access-date=2015-04-26}}{{cite web|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2015/5/25/5589852/michael-bisping-vs-thales-leites-main-event-set-july-18-ufc-fight-night-72-scotland-mma|title=Michael Bisping vs. Thales Leites main event set for UFC Fight Night 72 on July 18 in Scotland|publisher=mmamania|first=Jesse|last=Holland|date=2015-05-25|access-date=2015-05-25}}\n\n==Background==\nAfter previously hosting 15 events at various locations around the [[United Kingdom]], the event was the first that the promotion has hosted in [[Scotland]]. Ticket demand was high for the event, with all tickets selling out within a few hours of going on sale.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/06/scotlands-debut-event-ufc-fight-night-72-sells-out-in-a-matter-of-hours|title=Scotland's debut event, UFC Fight Night 72, sells out 'in a matter of hours'|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|access-date=2015-06-01|date=2015-06-01}}\n\nThe event was headlined by a middleweight bout between veteran contender [[Michael Bisping]] and former title challenger [[Thales Leites]].\n\n[[Ian Entwistle]] was expected to face [[Marcus Brimage]] at the event. However, Entwistle pulled out of the fight in late June for undisclosed reasons.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/06/ian-entwistle-out-at-ufc-fight-night-72-marcus-brimage-needs-new-opponent|title=Ian Entwistle out at UFC Fight Night 72, Marcus Brimage needs new opponent|publisher=mmajunkie.com|first=John|last=Morgan|date=2015-06-27|access-date=2015-06-27}} He was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Jimmie Rivera]].{{cite web|url=http://themmareport.com/2015/07/jimmie-rivera-signs-with-ufc-replaces-ian-entwistle-at-ufc-fight-night-72-and-meets-marcus-brimage/|title=Jimmie Rivera signs with UFC, replaces Ian Entwistle at UFC Fight Night 72 and meets Marcus Brimage|publisher=themmareport.com|author=Staff|date=2015-07-01|access-date=2015-07-01}}\n\nKonstantin Erokhin was expected to face [[Daniel Omielańczuk]] at the event. However, Erokhin pulled out of the fight in late June citing an injury and was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Chris de la Rocha]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/06/daniel-omielanczuk-gets-new-opponent-for-ufc-fight-night-72-in-scotland|title=Daniel Omielanczuk gets new opponent for UFC Fight Night 72 in Scotland|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-06-29|access-date=2015-06-29}}\n\n[[Jake Matthews (fighter)|Jake Matthews]] was expected to face [[Mickaël Lebout]] at the event. However, Matthews pulled out of the fight on 9 July due to an injury and was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Teemu Packalén]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/07/teemu-packalen-in-for-jake-matthews-against-mickael-lebout-at-ufc-fight-night-72|title=Teemu Packalen in for Jake Matthews against Mickael Lebout at UFC Fight Night 72|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-07-09|access-date=2015-07-09}}\n\n[[Bec Rawlings]] was expected to face [[Joanne Calderwood]] at the event. However, Rawlings pulled out of the fight on 10 July due to an injury and was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Cortney Casey]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/07/with-bec-rawlings-out-joanne-calderwood-gets-new-ufc-fight-night-72-opponent|title=With Bec Rawlings out, Joanne Calderwood gets new UFC Fight Night 72 opponent|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2015-07-10|access-date=2015-07-10}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (Fox Sports 1)}}\n{{MMAevent
